When\\n51: :setting:`DEBUG=False <DEBUG>` and :class:`~django.utils.log.AdminEmailHandler`\\n52: is configured in :setting:`LOGGING` (done by default), Django emails these\\n53: people the details of exceptions raised in the request/response cycle.\\n54: \\n55: Each item in the list should be an email address string. This is a security measure to prevent :ref:`HTTP Host header attacks\\n72: <host-headers-virtual-hosting>`, which are possible even under many\\n73: seemingly-safe web server configurations.\\n74: \\n75: Values in this list can be fully qualified names (e.g. ``'www.example.com'``),\\n76: in which case they will be matched against the request's ``Host`` header\\n77: exactly (case-insensitive, not including port). A value beginning with a period\\n78: can be used as a subdomain wildcard: ``'.example.com'`` will match\\n79: ``example.com``, ``www.example.com``, and any other subdomain of\\n80: ``example.com``. A value of ``'*'`` will match anything; in this case you are\\n81: responsible to provide your own validation of the ``Host`` header (perhaps in a\\n82: middleware; if so this middleware must be listed first in\\n83: :setting:`MIDDLEWARE`).\\n84: \\n85: Django also allows the `fully qualified domain name (FQDN)`_ of any entries.\\n86: Some browsers include a trailing dot in the ``Host`` header which Django\\n87: strips when performing host validation.\\n88: \\n89: .. _`fully qualified domain name (FQDN)`: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fully_qualified_domain_name\\n90: \\n91: If the ``Host`` header (or ``X-Forwarded-Host`` if\\n92: :setting:`USE_X_FORWARDED_HOST` is enabled) does not match any value in this\\n93: list, the :meth:`django.http.HttpRequest.get_host` method will raise\\n94: :exc:`~django.core.exceptions.SuspiciousOperation`.\\n95: \\n96: When :setting:`DEBUG` is ``True`` and ``ALLOWED_HOSTS`` is empty, the host\\n97: is validated against ``['.localhost', '127.0.0.1', '[::1]']``.\\n98: \\n99: ``ALLOWED_HOSTS`` is also :ref:`checked when running tests\\n100: <topics-testing-advanced-multiple-hosts>`.\\n101: \",\"url\":\"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/django/django@158fd81ef5a5647d27eb3065063284f9ee0a3ca4/-/blob/docs/ref/settings.txt%3FL42-100\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e80c5cf8-42e7-4815-81b0-43230d732032","uuid":"d83a5b7e-709a-4e5a-bdf5-b3e53d9ece53","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"content\":\"42: \\n43: .. setting:: ADMINS\\n44: \\n45: ``ADMINS``\\n46: ----------\\n47: \\n48: Default: ``[]`` (Empty list)\\n49: \\n50: A list of all the people who get code error notifications. This is a security measure to prevent :ref:`HTTP Host header attacks\\n72: <host-headers-virtual-hosting>`, which are possible even under many\\n73: seemingly-safe web server configurations.\\n74: \\n75: Values in this list can be fully qualified names (e.g. ``'www.example.com'``),\\n76: in which case they will be matched against the request's ``Host`` header\\n77: exactly (case-insensitive, not including port). A value beginning with a period\\n78: can be used as a subdomain wildcard: ``'.example.com'`` will match\\n79: ``example.com``, ``www.example.com``, and any other subdomain of\\n80: ``example.com``. A value of ``'*'`` will match anything; in this case you are\\n81: responsible to provide your own validation of the ``Host`` header (perhaps in a\\n82: middleware; if so this middleware must be listed first in\\n83: :setting:`MIDDLEWARE`).\\n84: \\n85: Django also allows the `fully qualified domain name (FQDN)`_ of any entries.\\n86: Some browsers include a trailing dot in the ``Host`` header which Django\\n87: strips when performing host validation.\\n88: \\n89: .. _`fully qualified domain name (FQDN)`: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fully_qualified_domain_name\\n90: \\n91: If the ``Host`` header (or ``X-Forwarded-Host`` if\\n92: :setting:`USE_X_FORWARDED_HOST` is enabled) does not match any value in this\\n93: list, the :meth:`django.http.HttpRequest.get_host` method will raise\\n94: :exc:`~django.core.exceptions.SuspiciousOperation`.\\n95: \\n96: When :setting:`DEBUG` is ``True`` and ``ALLOWED_HOSTS`` is empty, the host\\n97: is validated against ``['.localhost', '127.0.0.1', '[::1]']``.\\n98: \\n99: ``ALLOWED_HOSTS`` is also :ref:`checked when running tests\\n100: <topics-testing-advanced-multiple-hosts>`.\\n101: \",\"url\":\"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/django/django@158fd81ef5a5647d27eb3065063284f9ee0a3ca4/-/blob/docs/ref/settings.txt%3FL42-100\"}","structuredContent":{"content":"42: \n43: .. setting:: ADMINS\n44: \n45: ``ADMINS``\n46: ----------\n47: \n48: Default: ``[]`` (Empty list)\n49: \n50: A list of all the people who get code error notifications. When\n51: :setting:`DEBUG=False <DEBUG>` and :class:`~django.utils.log.AdminEmailHandler`\n52: is configured in :setting:`LOGGING` (done by default), Django emails these\n53: people the details of exceptions raised in the request/response cycle.\n54: \n55: Each item in the list should be an email address string. Example::\n56: \n57:     ADMINS = [\"john@example.com\", '\"Ng, Mary\" <mary@example.com>']\n58: \n59: .. versionchanged:: 6.0\n60: \n61:     In older versions, required a list of (name, address) tuples.\n62: \n63: .. setting:: ALLOWED_HOSTS\n64: \n65: ``ALLOWED_HOSTS``\n66: -----------------\n67: \n68: Default: ``[]`` (Empty list)\n69: \n70: A list of strings representing the host/domain names that this Django site can\n71: serve. This is a security measure to prevent :ref:`HTTP Host header attacks\n72: <host-headers-virtual-hosting>`, which are possible even under many\n73: seemingly-safe web server configurations.\n74: \n75: Values in this list can be fully qualified names (e.g. ``'www.example.com'``),\n76: in which case they will be matched against the request's ``Host`` header\n77: exactly (case-insensitive, not including port). A value beginning with a period\n78: can be used as a subdomain wildcard: ``'.example.com'`` will match\n79: ``example.com``, ``www.example.com``, and any other subdomain of\n80: ``example.com``. A value of ``'*'`` will match anything; in this case you are\n81: responsible to provide your own validation of the ``Host`` header (perhaps in a\n82: middleware; if so this middleware must be listed first in\n83: :setting:`MIDDLEWARE`).\n84: \n85: Django also allows the `fully qualified domain name (FQDN)`_ of any entries.\n86: Some browsers include a trailing dot in the ``Host`` header which Django\n87: strips when performing host validation.\n88: \n89: .. _`fully qualified domain name (FQDN)`: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fully_qualified_domain_name\n90: \n91: If the ``Host`` header (or ``X-Forwarded-Host`` if\n92: :setting:`USE_X_FORWARDED_HOST` is enabled) does not match any value in this\n93: list, the :meth:`django.http.HttpRequest.get_host` method will raise\n94: :exc:`~django.core.exceptions.SuspiciousOperation`.\n95: \n96: When :setting:`DEBUG` is ``True`` and ``ALLOWED_HOSTS`` is empty, the host\n97: is validated against ``['.localhost', '127.0.0.1', '[::1]']``.\n98: \n99: ``ALLOWED_HOSTS`` is also :ref:`checked when running tests\n100: <topics-testing-advanced-multiple-hosts>`.\n101: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/django/django@158fd81ef5a5647d27eb3065063284f9ee0a3ca4/-/blob/docs/ref/settings.txt%3FL42-100"}}}

This migration is already implemented as of Django 6.0, with backward compatibility maintained through Django 6.x and scheduled for full removal in Django 7.0.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe Django team determined that the name portion of the `(name, email)` tuple format was never used by Django's mail functions. The tuple format unnecessarily complicated the settings interface. The new format simply uses email address strings, allowing names to be included via standard email formatting conventions (e.g., `'\\\"Name\\\" <email@example.com>'`).\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Code Paths That Consume the Format\n\n#### 1. **Core Mail Functions** (`django/core/mail/__init__.py`)\n\n**Primary Implementation: `_send_server_message()` (lines 152-191)**\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(\n    *,\n    setting_name,\n    subject,\n    message,\n    html_message=None,\n    fail_silently=False,\n    connection=None,\n):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n\n    # RemovedInDjango70Warning.\n    if all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        warnings.warn(\n            f\"Using (name, address) pairs in the {setting_name} setting is deprecated.\"\n            \" Replace with a list of email address strings.\",\n            RemovedInDjango70Warning,\n            stacklevel=2,\n        )\n        recipients = [a[1] for a in recipients]\n\n    if not isinstance(recipients, (list, tuple)) or not all(\n        isinstance(address, (str, Promise)) for address in recipients\n    ):\n        raise ImproperlyConfigured(\n            f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of email address strings.\"\n        )\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Already migrated. Lines 166-173 handle backward compatibility by:\n- Detecting old tuple format via type checking\n- Issuing `RemovedInDjango70Warning` deprecation warning\n- Extracting email addresses from tuples via `[a[1] for a in recipients]`\n- Validating that final format is list of strings\n\n**Public API Functions:**\n- `mail_admins()` (lines 197-208): Calls `_send_server_message()` with setting_name=\"ADMINS\"\n- `mail_managers()` (lines 214-225): Calls `_send_server_message()` with setting_name=\"MANAGERS\"\n\n#### 2. **Error Logging Handler** (`django/utils/log.py`)\n\n**Location:** `AdminEmailHandler.emit()` (lines 94-101)\n```python\ndef emit(self, record):\n    # Early return when no email will be sent.\n    if (\n        not settings.ADMINS\n        # Method not overridden.\n        and self.send_mail.__func__ is AdminEmailHandler.send_mail\n    ):\n        return\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Only checks if ADMINS is empty. Delegates to `mail.mail_admins()` (line 138), which handles format validation.\n\n**Impact:** Used in default Django logging configuration to send 500-error notifications to admins.\n\n#### 3. **Middleware** (`django/middleware/common.py`)\n\n**Location:** `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware.process_response()` (line 131)\n```python\nmail_managers(\n    \"Broken %slink on %s\" % (...),\n    \"Referrer: ...\",\n    fail_silently=True,\n)\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Uses public `mail_managers()` API, which handles format validation.\n\n**Impact:** Sends broken link notifications to site managers.\n\n#### 4. **Management Command** (`django/core/management/commands/sendtestemail.py`)\n\n**Location:** Lines 42-46\n```python\nif kwargs[\"managers\"]:\n    mail_managers(subject, \"This email was sent to the site managers.\")\n\nif kwargs[\"admins\"]:\n    mail_admins(subject, \"This email was sent to the site admins.\")\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Uses public API functions that handle format validation.\n\n**Impact:** CLI tool for testing email configuration.\n\n---\n\n### Settings Definitions\n\n**Location:** `django/conf/global_settings.py`\n\n**ADMINS Setting (lines 24-26):**\n```python\n# People who get code error notifications. In the format\n# [\"email@example.com\", '\"Full Name\" <anotheremail@example.com>']\nADMINS = []\n```\n\n**MANAGERS Setting (lines 173-175):**\n```python\n# Not-necessarily-technical managers of the site. They get broken link\n# notifications and other various emails.\nMANAGERS = ADMINS\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Comments already show new string format. Default values are empty lists.\n\n---\n\n### Documentation References\n\n**1. Settings Documentation** (`docs/ref/settings.txt`)\n\n**ADMINS Setting (lines 42-61):**\n```\n``ADMINS``\n----------\n\nDefault: ``[]`` (Empty list)\n\nA list of all the people who get code error notifications. When\nDEBUG=False and AdminEmailHandler is configured in LOGGING (done by default),\nDjango emails these people the details of exceptions raised in the request/response cycle.\n\nEach item in the list should be an email address string. Example::\n\n    ADMINS = [\"john@example.com\", '\"Ng, Mary\" <mary@example.com>']\n\n.. versionchanged:: 6.0\n\n    In older versions, required a list of (name, address) tuples.\n```\n\n**MANAGERS Setting (lines 2074-2087):**\n```\n``MANAGERS``\n------------\n\nDefault: ``[]`` (Empty list)\n\nA list in the same format as ADMINS that specifies who should get\nbroken link notifications when BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware is enabled.\n\n.. versionchanged:: 6.0\n\n    In older versions, required a list of (name, address) tuples.\n```\n\n**2. Deprecation Documentation** (`docs/releases/6.0.txt`, lines 540-543)\n\n```\n* Setting :setting:`ADMINS` or :setting:`MANAGERS` to a list of (name, address)\n  tuples is deprecated. Set to a list of email address strings instead. Django\n  never used the name portion. To include a name, format the address string as\n  ``'\\\"Name\\\" <address>'`` or use Python's :func:`email.utils.formataddr`.\n```\n\n**3. This migration is already implemented as of Django 6.0, with backward compatibility maintained through Django 6.x and scheduled for full removal in Django 7.0.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe Django team determined that the name portion of the `(name, email)` tuple format was never used by Django's mail functions. The tuple format unnecessarily complicated the settings interface. The new format simply uses email address strings, allowing names to be included via standard email formatting conventions (e.g., `'\\\"Name\\\" <email@example.com>'`).\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Code Paths That Consume the Format\n\n#### 1. **Core Mail Functions** (`django/core/mail/__init__.py`)\n\n**Primary Implementation: `_send_server_message()` (lines 152-191)**\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(\n    *,\n    setting_name,\n    subject,\n    message,\n    html_message=None,\n    fail_silently=False,\n    connection=None,\n):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n\n    # RemovedInDjango70Warning.\n    if all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        warnings.warn(\n            f\"Using (name, address) pairs in the {setting_name} setting is deprecated.\"\n            \" Replace with a list of email address strings.\",\n            RemovedInDjango70Warning,\n            stacklevel=2,\n        )\n        recipients = [a[1] for a in recipients]\n\n    if not isinstance(recipients, (list, tuple)) or not all(\n        isinstance(address, (str, Promise)) for address in recipients\n    ):\n        raise ImproperlyConfigured(\n            f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of email address strings.\"\n        )\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Already migrated. Lines 166-173 handle backward compatibility by:\n- Detecting old tuple format via type checking\n- Issuing `RemovedInDjango70Warning` deprecation warning\n- Extracting email addresses from tuples via `[a[1] for a in recipients]`\n- Validating that final format is list of strings\n\n**Public API Functions:**\n- `mail_admins()` (lines 197-208): Calls `_send_server_message()` with setting_name=\"ADMINS\"\n- `mail_managers()` (lines 214-225): Calls `_send_server_message()` with setting_name=\"MANAGERS\"\n\n#### 2. **Error Logging Handler** (`django/utils/log.py`)\n\n**Location:** `AdminEmailHandler.emit()` (lines 94-101)\n```python\ndef emit(self, record):\n    # Early return when no email will be sent.\n    if (\n        not settings.ADMINS\n        # Method not overridden.\n        and self.send_mail.__func__ is AdminEmailHandler.send_mail\n    ):\n        return\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Only checks if ADMINS is empty. Delegates to `mail.mail_admins()` (line 138), which handles format validation.\n\n**Impact:** Used in default Django logging configuration to send 500-error notifications to admins.\n\n#### 3. **Middleware** (`django/middleware/common.py`)\n\n**Location:** `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware.process_response()` (line 131)\n```python\nmail_managers(\n    \"Broken %slink on %s\" % (...),\n    \"Referrer: ...\",\n    fail_silently=True,\n)\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Uses public `mail_managers()` API, which handles format validation.\n\n**Impact:** Sends broken link notifications to site managers.\n\n#### 4. **Management Command** (`django/core/management/commands/sendtestemail.py`)\n\n**Location:** Lines 42-46\n```python\nif kwargs[\"managers\"]:\n    mail_managers(subject, \"This email was sent to the site managers.\")\n\nif kwargs[\"admins\"]:\n    mail_admins(subject, \"This email was sent to the site admins.\")\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Uses public API functions that handle format validation.\n\n**Impact:** CLI tool for testing email configuration.\n\n---\n\n### Settings Definitions\n\n**Location:** `django/conf/global_settings.py`\n\n**ADMINS Setting (lines 24-26):**\n```python\n# People who get code error notifications. In the format\n# [\"email@example.com\", '\"Full Name\" <anotheremail@example.com>']\nADMINS = []\n```\n\n**MANAGERS Setting (lines 173-175):**\n```python\n# Not-necessarily-technical managers of the site. They get broken link\n# notifications and other various emails.\nMANAGERS = ADMINS\n```\n\n**Current Status:** ✅ Comments already show new string format. Default values are empty lists.\n\n---\n\n### Documentation References\n\n**1. Settings Documentation** (`docs/ref/settings.txt`)\n\n**ADMINS Setting (lines 42-61):**\n```\n``ADMINS``\n----------\n\nDefault: ``[]`` (Empty list)\n\nA list of all the people who get code error notifications. When\nDEBUG=False and AdminEmailHandler is configured in LOGGING (done by default),\nDjango emails these people the details of exceptions raised in the request/response cycle.\n\nEach item in the list should be an email address string. Example::\n\n    ADMINS = [\"john@example.com\", '\"Ng, Mary\" <mary@example.com>']\n\n.. versionchanged:: 6.0\n\n    In older versions, required a list of (name, address) tuples.\n```\n\n**MANAGERS Setting (lines 2074-2087):**\n```\n``MANAGERS``\n------------\n\nDefault: ``[]`` (Empty list)\n\nA list in the same format as ADMINS that specifies who should get\nbroken link notifications when BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware is enabled.\n\n.. versionchanged:: 6.0\n\n    In older versions, required a list of (name, address) tuples.\n```\n\n**2. Deprecation Documentation** (`docs/releases/6.0.txt`, lines 540-543)\n\n```\n* Setting :setting:`ADMINS` or :setting:`MANAGERS` to a list of (name, address)\n  tuples is deprecated. Set to a list of email address strings instead. Django\n  never used the name portion.
